Now tax time arrives.\"}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"3\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[\"And you face a problem: \",[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b24\",{\"children\":\"the IRS has issued zero formal guidance on how to report prediction market trades.\"}]]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"4\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":\"Not one.\"}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"5\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":\"This creates a dangerous knowledge gap. Unlike crypto trading (where Form 1099-DA clarifies how to report), or stock trading (where Form 1099-B does the job), prediction markets operate in regulatory gray space. The platforms may or may not send you a 1099, and even if they do, the IRS hasn't officially blessed any particular reporting method.\"}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"6\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":\"This post walks through three defensible tax treatments, the regulatory landscape, platform-specific notes, and the unique traps NJ residents face.\"}],[\"$\",\"h2\",\"7\",{\"id\":\"how-are-prediction-markets-regulated-and-why-does-that-affect-taxes\",\"className\":\"mb-4 mt-10 scroll-mt-24 font-serif text-h3 text-brand-blue dark:text-slate-100\",\"children\":\"How Are Prediction Markets Regulated and Why Does That Affect Taxes?\"}],[\"$\",\"h3\",\"8\",{\"id\":\"what-are-prediction-markets\",\"className\":\"mb-3 mt-8 scroll-mt-24 font-serif text-h4 text-brand-blue dark:text-slate-100\",\"children\":\"What Are Prediction Markets?\"}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"9\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":\"Prediction markets (also called event contracts) are financial instruments that settle based on real-world outcomes:\"}],[\"$\",\"ul\",\"ul-13\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 ml-6 list-disc space-y-1\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"li\",\"10\",{\"className\":\"text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Kalshi:\"}],\" Binary yes/no bets on events (Fed rate decisions, election outcomes, sports results, economic data, etc.). ~200+ active contracts at any time.\"]}],[\"$\",\"li\",\"11\",{\"className\":\"text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Polymarket:\"}],\" DeFi-native prediction market with a slightly more permissive regulatory posture toward traders outside the US (though US traders are permitted under specific conditions).\"]}],[\"$\",\"li\",\"12\",{\"className\":\"text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Robinhood Prediction Markets:\"}],\" Brand-new (launched Jan 2026). Binary outcomes, settled by Robinhood, launched with ~50 contracts on economics, politics, and sports.\"]}]]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"13\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":\"All three are binary: you buy a contract for $X at time T, the outcome resolves to YES (you get $1, maybe $0.99 on Robinhood due to fees) or NO (you get $0), and you pocket the difference (or loss).\"}],[\"$\",\"h3\",\"14\",{\"id\":\"why-the-regulatory-confusion\",\"className\":\"mb-3 mt-8 scroll-mt-24 font-serif text-h4 text-brand-blue dark:text-slate-100\",\"children\":\"Why the Regulatory Confusion?\"}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"15\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"The CFTC vs. SEC showdown.\"}],\" Kalshi is a CFTC-regulated exchange under the Dodd-Frank Act. It explicitly operates as a derivatives exchange and has specific exemptions for \\\"prediction contracts\\\" narrowly defined by size, outcome, and contract type. Polymarket and Robinhood are in grayer territory. Are they security exchanges (SEC), commodities exchanges (CFTC), or gambling-adjacent platforms (state regulators)?\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"16\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"The swap exclusion question.\"}],\" Dodd-Frank includes a \\\"swap exclusion\\\" that exempts certain small contracts from being classified as swaps. Kalshi bets are intentionally structured to qualify. But the IRS has never formally said what the tax treatment of a swap exclusion contract is -- is it a derivative? Is it gambling? Is it ordinary income?\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"17\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"State-level gambling laws.\"}],\" Even though the platforms claim their products aren't \\\"gambling\\\" (because the outcomes are objective, not games of chance), many states classify binary outcome contracts as wagers and subject them to gambling tax rules. NJ specifically has an \\\"insurance/wagering\\\" framework that creates unique traps.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"18\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"The 1099 inconsistency.\"}],\" Some platforms send traders 1099-MISC or 1099-B or nothing at all. The IRS hasn't clarified whether prediction market trades are reportable on Form 1099-DA (crypto), Form 1099-B (equities/derivatives), or Form 1040, Schedule C (business income) or Schedule 1 (miscellaneous).\"]}],[\"$\",\"h3\",\"19\",{\"id\":\"the-irs-stays-silent\",\"className\":\"mb-3 mt-8 scroll-mt-24 font-serif text-h4 text-brand-blue dark:text-slate-100\",\"children\":\"The IRS Stays Silent\"}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"20\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":\"As of March 1, 2026, the IRS has not issued a Revenue Ruling or Private Letter Ruling on prediction market taxation, has not clarified whether prediction markets are securities, derivatives, or something else, has not defined whether traders should use Section 1256 treatment (60/40 long-term/short-term) or ordinary income rates, and has not clarified the CFTC swap exclusion's tax implications. The IRS has implicitly acknowledged prediction markets exist through various enforcement actions and CFTC coordination.\"}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"21\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":\"This silence forces traders and CPAs into an interpretive vacuum. The three approaches below are the most defensible, but none is bulletproof.\"}],[\"$\",\"h2\",\"22\",{\"id\":\"what-are-the-three-possible-tax-treatments-for-prediction-markets-ranked-by-defensibility\",\"className\":\"mb-4 mt-10 scroll-mt-24 font-serif text-h3 text-brand-blue dark:text-slate-100\",\"children\":\"What Are the Three Possible Tax Treatments for Prediction Markets (Ranked by Defensibility)?\"}],[\"$\",\"h3\",\"23\",{\"id\":\"approach-1-speculative-derivatives-section-1256-most-aggressive\",\"className\":\"mb-3 mt-8 scroll-mt-24 font-serif text-h4 text-brand-blue dark:text-slate-100\",\"children\":\"Approach 1: Speculative Derivatives (Section 1256) -- Most Aggressive\"}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"24\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"What it is:\"}],\" You classify your prediction market positions as Section 1256 contracts (commodities derivatives regulated by the CFTC).\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"25\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"How it works:\"}],\" All gains and losses are aggregated for the year. 60% of the net gain is treated as long-term capital gain (taxed at favorable rates). 40% is treated as short-term capital gain (taxed at ordinary income rates). This is regardless of your holding period. Even if you hold the contract for one day, you get the 60/40 split. Form 8949 is not used; instead, you report it on Schedule D under \\\"Section 1256 contracts.\\\"\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"26\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Example:\"}],\" You net $10,000 in prediction market gains for 2025. 60% ($6,000) is taxed as long-term cap gain. 40% ($4,000) is taxed as short-term cap gain. On a $10,000 net gain, your tax bill is ~$2,100 (assuming 24% marginal rate and 20% LTCG rate).\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"27\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Why it's defensible:\"}],\" Kalshi contracts are explicitly exempted from being \\\"swaps\\\" under Dodd-Frank and the CFTC rules. Section 1256 applies to commodities contracts traded on CFTC-regulated exchanges. Kalshi is a CFTC exchange, so the argument is: \\\"These are Section 1256 derivatives, period.\\\" Some tax attorneys and crypto-focused CPAs have been quietly using this method.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"28\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Why it's risky:\"}],\" The IRS has not formally blessed this approach. If the IRS decides prediction markets are not Section 1256 contracts, you could owe additional tax, penalties, and interest. The swap exclusion was designed to exempt prediction contracts from Dodd-Frank's swap regulation, not to define their tax treatment. Robinhood and Polymarket are not CFTC exchanges in the same way Kalshi is. The CFTC's regulatory framework and the IRS's tax framework are not aligned.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"29\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Who it suits:\"}],\" High-volume traders who realize large net gains and want to minimize their tax rate. Kalshi traders specifically (Kalshi is explicitly CFTC-regulated).\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"30\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Red flag:\"}],\" If you get audited and the IRS challenges this, you'd likely lose and owe back taxes plus penalties. I would not recommend this without a written opinion letter from a tax attorney.\"]}],[\"$\",\"h3\",\"31\",{\"id\":\"approach-2-gambling-gaming-income-ordinary-income-moderate-conservative\",\"className\":\"mb-3 mt-8 scroll-mt-24 font-serif text-h4 text-brand-blue dark:text-slate-100\",\"children\":\"Approach 2: Gambling/Gaming Income (Ordinary Income) -- Moderate Conservative\"}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"32\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"What it is:\"}],\" You classify your prediction market trading as gambling income, subject to the hobby loss rules and gaming taxes.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"33\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"How it works:\"}],\" All wins are reported as taxable income on Schedule 1, Line 8b (Gambling Income) or Schedule C (if you're a professional gambler). Losses are deductible on Schedule A, Line 16 if you itemize, up to the amount of your winnings. Starting in 2026, the OBBBA caps the loss deduction at 90% of total losses. If you're a professional gambler, losses are deducted as business expenses on Schedule C.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"34\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[\"The key question: Are you a \\\"professional-gambler\\\" or an amateur? \",[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b66\",{\"children\":\"Professional:\"}],\" You spend significant time, have trading records, pursue it like a business. Your net gains over time. \",[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b187\",{\"children\":\"Amateur:\"}],\" Casual, you play for fun, you lack a systematic approach.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"35\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":\"If you're a professional gambler, you file Schedule C and can deduct all losses as business expenses. If you're an amateur, you report winnings on Schedule 1, Line 8b and deduct losses on Schedule A, Line 16 if you itemize, up to the amount of your winnings. Starting in 2026, the OBBBA caps the loss deduction at 90% of total losses. If you take the standard deduction, you cannot deduct any gambling losses.\"}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"36\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Example:\"}],\" You have $50,000 in prediction market wins and $40,000 in losses in 2025. If you're a professional gambler: You report on Schedule C, deduct your $40,000 in losses, and pay tax on the $10,000 net. If you're an amateur who itemizes: You report $50,000 in winnings on Schedule 1, Line 8b and deduct $40,000 in losses on Schedule A, Line 16. Your taxable gambling income is $10,000. For 2026 activity, the loss deduction would be capped at $36,000 (90% of $40,000), leaving $14,000 taxable. If you take the standard deduction, you cannot deduct any losses and owe tax on the full $50,000.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"37\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Why it's defensible:\"}],\" Gambling winnings are taxable income under IRC Section 61. Prediction markets have binary outcomes based on real-world events -- this resembles wagering, not investment. Many states, including NJ, classify prediction markets as \\\"wagers\\\" or \\\"gaming activity.\\\" The IRS has long experience auditing gambling income.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"38\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Why it's risky:\"}],\" If the IRS takes the position that prediction markets are not \\\"gambling\\\" but rather speculative derivatives or ordinary business income, you might have reported too much. The gambling framework is less favorable for large traders because losses are harder to deduct.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"39\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Who it suits:\"}],\" Conservative traders who want alignment with state-level gambling classifications. Small-to-medium traders. Traders in states where gambling is heavily regulated (e.g., NJ, NY).\"]}],[\"$\",\"h3\",\"40\",{\"id\":\"approach-3-taxable-event-ordinary-business-income-most-conservative\",\"className\":\"mb-3 mt-8 scroll-mt-24 font-serif text-h4 text-brand-blue dark:text-slate-100\",\"children\":\"Approach 3: Taxable Event / Ordinary Business Income -- Most Conservative\"}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"41\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"What it is:\"}],\" You report prediction market gains as miscellaneous ordinary income, filed as Schedule 1 income (other income) or Schedule C (if you're in the business of trading).\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"42\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"How it works:\"}],\" Each win (when a contract settles in your favor) is reported as ordinary income. Each loss (when a contract settles against you) is reported as a loss. If you're not a business, you report the net on Schedule 1, Line 8z (Other Income). If you are a business, you file Schedule C and claim all losses as business deductions. This is the most straightforward, least aggressive approach.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"43\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Example:\"}],\" You net $10,000 in prediction market gains in 2025. You report it on Schedule 1 as \\\"Other Income.\\\" You pay tax at your marginal rate (~24%), i.e., ~$2,400.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"44\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Why it's defensible:\"}],\" The IRS taxes all income from any source unless specifically excluded by law. This approach doesn't require you to make a theory about whether they're derivatives or gambling. You're just reporting them as income. This is what the vast majority of traders and CPAs are likely to do by default.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"45\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Why it's safe:\"}],\" You're taking the most conservative position. The IRS is unlikely to challenge you for reporting too much income. It's consistent with the principle of \\\"when in doubt, report more income and claim fewer deductions.\\\"\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"46\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Who it suits:\"}],\" Traders who prefer certainty over optimization. Traders with small net gains or losses. Traders who are not confident in their ability to defend a more aggressive position.\"]}],[\"$\",\"h2\",\"47\",{\"id\":\"how-does-each-prediction-market-platform-handle-tax-reporting\",\"className\":\"mb-4 mt-10 scroll-mt-24 font-serif text-h3 text-brand-blue dark:text-slate-100\",\"children\":\"How Does Each Prediction Market Platform Handle Tax Reporting?\"}],[\"$\",\"h3\",\"48\",{\"id\":\"kalshi\",\"className\":\"mb-3 mt-8 scroll-mt-24 font-serif text-h4 text-brand-blue dark:text-slate-100\",\"children\":\"Kalshi\"}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"49\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Scale:\"}],\" Kalshi posted $23.8 billion in total 2025 trading volume (1,108% year-over-year increase). As of early March 2026, rolling 30-day volume sits at approximately $6.7 billion with open interest of $433.6 million. Sports contracts represent 85-92% of volume.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"50\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Regulatory status:\"}],\" CFTC Designated Contract Market. Kalshi appears on EY's official Section 1256 qualified board or exchange list. However, the CFTC itself classifies event contracts as \\\"binary options\\\" categorized as \\\"swaps\\\" under 7 U.S.C. Section 1a(47). This swap classification may trigger the Dodd-Frank exclusion under IRC Section 1256(b)(2)(B) that Congress enacted to prevent such contracts from receiving 60/40 treatment.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"51\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Tax reporting:\"}],\" Kalshi issues 1099-INT for interest earned on cash balances (at or above $10), 1099-MISC for referral bonuses or credits (at or above $600), and a limited 1099-B for crypto transfer transactions only. Kalshi does \",[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b232\",{\"children\":\"not\"}],\" issue a 1099-B for event contract trades. You must self-report. Kalshi provides year-end trade history and P\u0026L reports. Kalshi uses FIFO accounting for its P\u0026L display but explicitly disclaims this as tax advice.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"52\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Fee structure (updated February 5, 2026):\"}],\" Taker fees follow the formula 0.07 x C x P x (1-P), producing a maximum of approximately $0.02 per contract at the $0.50 midpoint. Maker fees are one-quarter of the taker rate. Kalshi pays up to 4.05% APY on idle cash balances.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"53\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Why Kalshi traders might prefer Approach 1:\"}],\" Kalshi's explicit CFTC DCM registration and presence on EY's Section 1256 QBE list is the strongest factual support for the Section 1256 argument.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"54\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Risk:\"}],\" The CFTC's own swap classification creates a strong argument against Section 1256 eligibility. In its February 2026 amicus brief in \",[\"$\",\"em\",\"i142\",{\"children\":\"North American Derivatives Exchange v. State of Nevada\"}],\", the CFTC argued that event contracts on CFTC-registered DCMs are swaps under the CEA. The IRS has consistently taken a narrow view of Section 1256 categories (IRS Notice 2007-71, \",[\"$\",\"em\",\"i379\",{\"children\":\"Summitt v. Commissioner\"}],\" 134 T.C. 248, \",[\"$\",\"em\",\"i419\",{\"children\":\"Wright v. Commissioner\"}],\" 809 F.3d 877). File Form 8275 if taking this position.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"55\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"State restrictions:\"}],\" 9 states have issued cease-and-desist letters to Kalshi (NJ, NY, NV, MA, MD, OH, MT, AZ, AR, IL). Massachusetts issued a preliminary injunction (January 2026, currently stayed on appeal). Federal courts are split on whether CFTC preemption overrides state gambling laws. A coalition of 39 state attorneys general plus D.C. has filed amicus briefs against Kalshi.\"]}],[\"$\",\"h3\",\"56\",{\"id\":\"polymarket\",\"className\":\"mb-3 mt-8 scroll-mt-24 font-serif text-h4 text-brand-blue dark:text-slate-100\",\"children\":\"Polymarket\"}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"57\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Two platforms now exist:\"}],\" Polymarket International continues to operate at massive scale (smart contracts operated by Blockratize, Inc.). Polymarket US (QCX LLC d/b/a Polymarket US) launched in beta on November 12, 2025 via a $112 million acquisition of QCEX, and operates as a CFTC-registered DCM requiring full KYC. As of March 2026, Polymarket US remains invite-only with 6-12 week waits for new signups.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"58\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Volume caveat:\"}],\" A December 2025 Paradigm Research report revealed that most Polymarket dashboards have been double-counting volume due to how on-chain OrderFilled events work. Reported figures may be roughly twice the actual volume.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"59\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Tax reporting:\"}],\" Polymarket International issues no tax forms. Polymarket US is expected to issue 1099 forms once past beta, but this has not been confirmed. For International trades, you must reconstruct P\u0026L from blockchain records.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"60\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Polymarket International (offshore):\"}],\" The US is among 33 completely blocked countries. VPN use is prohibited under Section 2.1.4 of the Terms of Service. The Section 1256 argument is significantly weaker for offshore trades, as Polymarket International is not a CFTC-regulated exchange. FBAR and FATCA reporting obligations may apply (see the \",[\"$\",\"a\",\"a346\",{\"href\":\"/prediction-market-tax\",\"className\":\"text-brand-gold underline transition-colors hover:text-brand-gold/80\",\"children\":\"prediction market tax services page\"}],\" for details).\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"61\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Polymarket US (beta):\"}],\" Because Polymarket US is a CFTC-regulated DCM, the Section 1256 argument is stronger here than for International trades (similar to Kalshi). Available markets are more limited: sports, crypto, and entertainment. Political/election markets are delayed pending further CFTC review.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"62\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Recommendation:\"}],\" Approach 2 (gambling) or Approach 3 (ordinary income) is more defensible for Polymarket International trades. For Polymarket US trades, the analysis is similar to Kalshi.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"63\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Taker fees:\"}],\" Polymarket introduced taker fees on select crypto and sports markets in early 2026. Polymarket US charges a 0.10% flat taker fee.\"]}],[\"$\",\"h3\",\"64\",{\"id\":\"robinhood-prediction-markets\",\"className\":\"mb-3 mt-8 scroll-mt-24 font-serif text-h4 text-brand-blue dark:text-slate-100\",\"children\":\"Robinhood Prediction Markets\"}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"65\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Scale:\"}],\" Over 1 million customers have traded prediction markets on Robinhood. CEO Vlad Tenev reported on the February 2026 earnings call that 12+ billion contracts were traded in 2025, with 4 billion already in 2026. Prediction markets are Robinhood's fastest-growing product line by revenue, on pace for $300+ million annualized.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"66\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Regulatory status:\"}],\" Robinhood routes orders through Robinhood Derivatives, LLC (a CFTC-registered Futures Commission Merchant) to Kalshi's exchange. Robinhood is preparing to leave Kalshi's exchange: in January 2026, it closed its acquisition of MIAXdx through a joint venture with Susquehanna International Group called Rothera, giving Robinhood its own CFTC-licensed DCM and clearinghouse. This exchange is expected to begin operations in 2026.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"67\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Tax reporting:\"}],\" Robinhood explicitly states \\\"Robinhood will not be providing 1099s for event contract trades.\\\" Robinhood provides an Event Contracts Annual Statement summarizing trades, but labels it \\\"not a substitute tax reporting form.\\\" Robinhood charges $0.01 per contract per side as commission, on top of Kalshi's exchange fees, for approximately $0.02 per contract total.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"68\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Recommendation:\"}],\" Use the same analysis as Kalshi (since Robinhood trades execute on Kalshi's infrastructure). Robinhood is restricted in Maryland (no event contracts at all) and Nevada/New Jersey (no sports contracts). File conservatively and amend later if the IRS clarifies.\"]}],[\"$\",\"h3\",\"69\",{\"id\":\"predictit\",\"className\":\"mb-3 mt-8 scroll-mt-24 font-serif text-h4 text-brand-blue dark:text-slate-100\",\"children\":\"PredictIt\"}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"70\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":\"PredictIt survived its CFTC challenge with final judgment in its favor on July 22, 2025. The $850 per-contract position limit has been raised to $3,500 (indexed to the Federal Election Campaign Act limit under CFTC Letter No. 25-20). The 5,000-trader-per-contract cap was eliminated. PredictIt charges 10% on gross profits plus a 5% withdrawal fee (the highest fee structure in the industry). PredictIt remains restricted to political event contracts only. PredictIt issues 1099-MISC for net winnings above $600.\"}],[\"$\",\"h2\",\"71\",{\"id\":\"how-do-states-like-new-jersey-tax-prediction-market-income\",\"className\":\"mb-4 mt-10 scroll-mt-24 font-serif text-h3 text-brand-blue dark:text-slate-100\",\"children\":\"How Do States Like New Jersey Tax Prediction Market Income?\"}],[\"$\",\"h3\",\"72\",{\"id\":\"federal-vs-state-tax-treatment\",\"className\":\"mb-3 mt-8 scroll-mt-24 font-serif text-h4 text-brand-blue dark:text-slate-100\",\"children\":\"Federal vs. State Tax Treatment\"}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"73\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":\"The federal IRS framework above doesn't prevent states from imposing their own taxes on prediction market activity. Some states treat prediction markets as gambling and impose a wagering tax (e.g., New Jersey, Nevada, Illinois). Others treat them as capital gains and apply state income tax. Others treat them as business income and require a state license or business registration.\"}],[\"$\",\"h3\",\"74\",{\"id\":\"new-jersey-specifics\",\"className\":\"mb-3 mt-8 scroll-mt-24 font-serif text-h4 text-brand-blue dark:text-slate-100\",\"children\":\"New Jersey Specifics\"}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"75\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":\"If you're a NJ resident, pay close attention. NJ has a historically strict gambling and insurance regulatory framework that creates unique traps for prediction market traders.\"}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"76\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"The NJ Insurance/Wagering Distinction.\"}],\" NJ classifies contracts on uncertain future events in two buckets: (1) Insurance, where you have an insurable interest and the contract indemnifies you against loss, and (2) Wagering, where you have no insurable interest and the contract is purely speculative. Prediction markets are almost certainly wagering under NJ law, not insurance.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"77\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"NJ Gambling Winnings.\"}],\" Professional gamblers file Schedule C (business income) and deduct losses. Casual gamblers file ordinary income tax on net winnings; losses are not deductible beyond winnings. NJ allows full netting of wins and losses (unlike federal, which caps at 90% for 2026+).\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"78\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"The Trap for NJ Residents.\"}],\" If you use Approach 1 (Section 1256) federally, you get favorable 60/40 rates on your federal return. But NJ may not recognize Section 1256 treatment for prediction markets and could tax the full amount at ordinary income rates (up to 10.75%). You could pay federal tax at favorable rates and NJ tax at unfavorable rates.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"79\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Example:\"}],\" NJ resident realizes $10,000 net gain on Kalshi. Federal: Uses Approach 1, taxes $6,000 at 20% (LTCG) and $4,000 at 24% (short-term). Total federal = ~$2,100. NJ: Taxes $10,000 at ordinary income rates (~4-10.75% depending on bracket). Total NJ = ~$850. Total tax: ~$2,950 (effective rate ~29.5%). Compare to a resident of Florida or Texas: Total tax: ~$2,100 (effective rate ~21%). NJ residents are penalized by ~$850 for each $10,000 in gains.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"80\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"What NJ Traders Should Do.\"}],\" Track gross winnings and losses separately -- you may need this for NJ reporting. Consult a NJ-licensed CPA before filing. Consider the state tax impact when deciding between Approaches 1, 2, and 3. File Form NJ-1040 with itemized deductions if you have substantial losses.\"]}],[\"$\",\"h2\",\"81\",{\"id\":\"how-do-i-actually-file-my-taxes-for-each-prediction-market-tax-approach\",\"className\":\"mb-4 mt-10 scroll-mt-24 font-serif text-h3 text-brand-blue dark:text-slate-100\",\"children\":\"How Do I Actually File My Taxes for Each Prediction Market Tax Approach?\"}],[\"$\",\"h3\",\"82\",{\"id\":\"if-you-choose-approach-1-section-1256-derivatives\",\"className\":\"mb-3 mt-8 scroll-mt-24 font-serif text-h4 text-brand-blue dark:text-slate-100\",\"children\":\"If You Choose Approach 1: Section 1256 Derivatives\"}],[\"$\",\"ol\",\"ol-87\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 ml-6 list-decimal space-y-1\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"li\",\"83\",{\"className\":\"text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":\"Calculate your net P\u0026L for the year from all prediction market trades.\"}],[\"$\",\"li\",\"84\",{\"className\":\"text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":\"On Schedule D (Capital Gains and Losses), go to Part III: \\\"Gain from Form 6781, Section 1256 Contracts and Straddles.\\\" Enter your unrealized and realized gains/losses. Do NOT use Form 8949. The 60/40 split is automatic.\"}],[\"$\",\"li\",\"85\",{\"className\":\"text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":\"Report 60% as long-term capital gain and 40% as short-term capital gain.\"}],[\"$\",\"li\",\"86\",{\"className\":\"text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":\"For NJ residents: File Schedule A and note the state tax impact. You may owe ordinary income tax on the full amount in NJ even though you reported long-term gains federally.\"}]]}],[\"$\",\"h3\",\"87\",{\"id\":\"if-you-choose-approach-2-gambling-income\",\"className\":\"mb-3 mt-8 scroll-mt-24 font-serif text-h4 text-brand-blue dark:text-slate-100\",\"children\":\"If You Choose Approach 2: Gambling Income\"}],[\"$\",\"ol\",\"ol-92\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 ml-6 list-decimal space-y-1\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"li\",\"88\",{\"className\":\"text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":\"Calculate your gross winnings and gross losses separately.\"}],[\"$\",\"li\",\"89\",{\"className\":\"text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":\"Determine if you're a \\\"professional gambler.\\\" If yes: File Schedule C (Business Income and Loss). Report gross winnings as income and deduct all losses as business expenses. If no: Report gross winnings on Schedule 1, Line 8b. Deduct losses on Schedule A, Line 16 if you itemize (up to the amount of winnings; capped at 90% of losses starting 2026). If you take the standard deduction, losses are not deductible.\"}],[\"$\",\"li\",\"90\",{\"className\":\"text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":\"On Schedule 1, Line 8b: \\\"Gambling income.\\\" Enter your gross gambling winnings. Deduct losses on Schedule A, Line 16 if you itemize.\"}],[\"$\",\"li\",\"91\",{\"className\":\"text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":\"For NJ residents: NJ allows full 100% netting of gambling wins and losses on the NJ-1040 regardless of the federal 90% cap. Report net gambling income on NJ-1040.\"}]]}],[\"$\",\"h3\",\"92\",{\"id\":\"if-you-choose-approach-3-ordinary-income-schedule-1-or-c\",\"className\":\"mb-3 mt-8 scroll-mt-24 font-serif text-h4 text-brand-blue dark:text-slate-100\",\"children\":\"If You Choose Approach 3: Ordinary Income (Schedule 1 or C)\"}],[\"$\",\"ol\",\"ol-96\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 ml-6 list-decimal space-y-1\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"li\",\"93\",{\"className\":\"text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":\"Calculate your net P\u0026L for the year.\"}],[\"$\",\"li\",\"94\",{\"className\":\"text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":\"On Schedule 1 or C: If Schedule 1, report the net as \\\"Other Income.\\\" If Schedule C, report it as business income and deduct all losses as business expenses.\"}],[\"$\",\"li\",\"95\",{\"className\":\"text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":\"For NJ residents: Same as Approach 2, but note that you're treating it as miscellaneous income, not gambling income. This is the least defensive position for state purposes, but the safest federally.\"}]]}],[\"$\",\"h2\",\"96\",{\"id\":\"what-records-do-i-need-to-track-for-prediction-market-taxes\",\"className\":\"mb-4 mt-10 scroll-mt-24 font-serif text-h3 text-brand-blue dark:text-slate-100\",\"children\":\"What Records Do I Need to Track for Prediction Market Taxes?\"}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"97\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[\"Regardless of which approach you choose, \",[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b41\",{\"children\":\"document everything:\"}]]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"98\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Trade history.\"}],\" Date of purchase, contract ID (e.g., Kalshi contract number), amount invested, date of settlement/sale, amount received (or lost), and net P\u0026L.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"99\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Platform statements.\"}],\" Year-end P\u0026L report from Kalshi, Polymarket, or Robinhood. Monthly trade confirmations (if available). Any 1099 forms sent by the platform.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"100\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Intent and records.\"}],\" Document whether you're trading as a hobby, professional gambler, or business. This is a defense against penalties if the IRS later clarifies the treatment differently.\"]}],[\"$\",\"h2\",\"103\",{\"id\":\"my-recommendation\",\"className\":\"mb-4 mt-10 scroll-mt-24 font-serif text-h3 text-brand-blue dark:text-slate-100\",\"children\":\"My Recommendation\"}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"104\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":\"The IRS has left a dangerous gap in guidance on prediction market taxation. Use it only if you're a high-volume Kalshi trader and comfortable with potential amendments.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"106\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Approach 2 (gambling income)\"}],\" aligns with state-level classifications and is consistent with how the IRS treats similar wagering. Use it if you're a semi-professional trader or in a state with strong gambling tax rules (like NJ).\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"107\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"Approach 3 (ordinary income)\"}],\" is the most conservative. Use it if you want to minimize audit risk and don't mind paying the extra tax now.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"108\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"For NJ residents:\"}],\" Factor in state taxes. Approach 1 looks great federally but may be taxed at high rates in NJ. Approach 2 is more aligned with NJ's wagering framework. Consult a NJ CPA before filing.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"109\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"My recommendation:\"}],\" Unless you're a high-volume Kalshi trader with a CPA or tax attorney advising you, use \",[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b110\",{\"children\":\"Approach 3 (ordinary income on Schedule 1 or C)\"}],\". It's the safest and most defensible. You'll pay more federal tax now, but you'll avoid audit risk and penalties. If the IRS later clarifies that Approach 1 is correct, you can file an amended return and claim a refund of the overpayment.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"110\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"b0\",{\"children\":\"For 2026 filing:\"}],\" The situation may change. Watch for new IRS guidance (Revenue Ruling, Notice, or FAQ), CFTC clarification on Polymarket and Robinhood's regulatory status, state-level guidance from NJ or other high-tax states, and platform 1099 policies. File conservatively, document well, and be ready to amend if guidance clarifies.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"111\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[\"Readers uncertain whether prediction markets are \\\"gambling\\\" or \\\"investment income\\\" should read our \",[\"$\",\"a\",\"a99\",{\"href\":\"/gambling-tax\",\"className\":\"text-brand-gold underline transition-colors hover:text-brand-gold/80\",\"children\":\"gambling tax services page\"}],\", which walks through the distinction and covers the \\\"professional trader\\\" test in detail.\"]}],[\"$\",\"h2\",\"112\",{\"id\":\"key-takeaway\",\"className\":\"mb-4 mt-10 scroll-mt-24 font-serif text-h3 text-brand-blue dark:text-slate-100\",\"children\":\"Key Takeaway\"}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"113\",{\"className\":\"mb-5 text-[16px] leading-[1.8] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300\",\"children\":[\"The IRS has issued zero formal guidance on prediction market taxation, leaving traders to choose between three defensible approaches with wildly different tax consequences. Section 1256 treatment is the most aggressive but tax-efficient; gambling income aligns with state classifications; ordinary income is the safest. NJ residents face unique traps regardless of which approach they choose. Document everything, consider filing Form 8275 if you take a non-obvious position, and work with a CPA who understands both federal and NJ rules. Kalshi has said it will not send 1099s initially; Robinhood has not clarified; Polymarket doesn't send them (it's a DeFi platform). If you don't get a 1099, you must still self-report your income. The IRS can audit you for unreported gains even without a 1099.\"}]]}]]}],[\"$\",\"details\",\"faq-122\",{\"className\":\"faq-item group mb-2 rounded-xl border border-gray-200 open:border-brand-gold/30 dark:border-slate-700 dark:open:border-brand-gold/20\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"summary\",null,{\"className\":\"flex cursor-pointer list-none items-start justify-between gap-3 px-5 py-4 font-sans text-[15.5px] font-semibold text-brand-blue hover:text-brand-gold dark:text-slate-100 dark:hover:text-brand-gold\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"span\",null,{\"children\":\"Is prediction market income gambling or capital gains?\"}],[\"$\",\"svg\",null,{\"xmlns\":\"http://www.w3.org/2000/svg\",\"width\":24,\"height\":24,\"viewBox\":\"0 0 24 24\",\"fill\":\"none\",\"stroke\":\"currentColor\",\"strokeWidth\":2,\"strokeLinecap\":\"round\",\"strokeLinejoin\":\"round\",\"className\":\"lucide lucide-chevron-down faq-chevron mt-0.5 h-4 w-4 flex-shrink-0 text-brand-muted transition-transform duration-200\",\"aria-hidden\":\"true\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"path\",\"qrunsl\",{\"d\":\"m6 9 6 6 6-6\"}],\"$undefined\"]}]]}],[\"$\",\"div\",null,{\"className\":\"border-t border-gray-100 px-5 pb-5 pt-4 dark:border-slate-700/50\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"p\",\"faqp-121\",{\"className\":\"text-[15px] leading-[1.7] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300 [\u0026:not(:last-child)]:mb-2\",\"children\":\"The IRS has not clarified. Three possible treatments exist: Section 1256 derivatives (60/40 long-term/short-term split), gambling income (ordinary rates, loss deduction limitations), and ordinary income (most conservative). Each has different tax consequences. The classification you choose affects your federal tax rate, your loss deduction ability, and your NJ state tax treatment.\"}]]}]]}],[\"$\",\"details\",\"faq-124\",{\"className\":\"faq-item group mb-2 rounded-xl border border-gray-200 open:border-brand-gold/30 dark:border-slate-700 dark:open:border-brand-gold/20\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"summary\",null,{\"className\":\"flex cursor-pointer list-none items-start justify-between gap-3 px-5 py-4 font-sans text-[15.5px] font-semibold text-brand-blue hover:text-brand-gold dark:text-slate-100 dark:hover:text-brand-gold\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"span\",null,{\"children\":\"Does the 90% gambling loss cap apply to my 2025 return?\"}],[\"$\",\"svg\",null,{\"xmlns\":\"http://www.w3.org/2000/svg\",\"width\":24,\"height\":24,\"viewBox\":\"0 0 24 24\",\"fill\":\"none\",\"stroke\":\"currentColor\",\"strokeWidth\":2,\"strokeLinecap\":\"round\",\"strokeLinejoin\":\"round\",\"className\":\"lucide lucide-chevron-down faq-chevron mt-0.5 h-4 w-4 flex-shrink-0 text-brand-muted transition-transform duration-200\",\"aria-hidden\":\"true\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"path\",\"qrunsl\",{\"d\":\"m6 9 6 6 6-6\"}],\"$undefined\"]}]]}],[\"$\",\"div\",null,{\"className\":\"border-t border-gray-100 px-5 pb-5 pt-4 dark:border-slate-700/50\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"p\",\"faqp-123\",{\"className\":\"text-[15px] leading-[1.7] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300 [\u0026:not(:last-child)]:mb-2\",\"children\":[\"No. The 90% cap under the OBBBA's amendment to Section 165(d) takes effect January 1, 2026. Your 2025 return (being filed now) still uses the old 100% loss deduction rule. However, if you classify prediction market income as gambling and continue trading in 2026, the 90% cap will apply to your 2026 activity. \",[\"$\",\"a\",\"a310\",{\"href\":\"/post/90-percent-gambling-loss-cap\",\"className\":\"text-brand-gold underline transition-colors hover:text-brand-gold/80\",\"children\":\"Read the full 90% cap analysis\"}]]}]]}]]}],[\"$\",\"details\",\"faq-126\",{\"className\":\"faq-item group mb-2 rounded-xl border border-gray-200 open:border-brand-gold/30 dark:border-slate-700 dark:open:border-brand-gold/20\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"summary\",null,{\"className\":\"flex cursor-pointer list-none items-start justify-between gap-3 px-5 py-4 font-sans text-[15.5px] font-semibold text-brand-blue hover:text-brand-gold dark:text-slate-100 dark:hover:text-brand-gold\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"span\",null,{\"children\":\"What is the Section 1256 swap exclusion and why does it matter?\"}],[\"$\",\"svg\",null,{\"xmlns\":\"http://www.w3.org/2000/svg\",\"width\":24,\"height\":24,\"viewBox\":\"0 0 24 24\",\"fill\":\"none\",\"stroke\":\"currentColor\",\"strokeWidth\":2,\"strokeLinecap\":\"round\",\"strokeLinejoin\":\"round\",\"className\":\"lucide lucide-chevron-down faq-chevron mt-0.5 h-4 w-4 flex-shrink-0 text-brand-muted transition-transform duration-200\",\"aria-hidden\":\"true\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"path\",\"qrunsl\",{\"d\":\"m6 9 6 6 6-6\"}],\"$undefined\"]}]]}],[\"$\",\"div\",null,{\"className\":\"border-t border-gray-100 px-5 pb-5 pt-4 dark:border-slate-700/50\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"p\",\"faqp-125\",{\"className\":\"text-[15px] leading-[1.7] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300 [\u0026:not(:last-child)]:mb-2\",\"children\":\"The Dodd-Frank Act includes a \\\"swap exclusion\\\" that exempts certain small prediction contracts from being classified as regulated swaps. Kalshi uses this exclusion to operate. Some practitioners argue this means Kalshi contracts qualify for Section 1256 tax treatment (60/40 long-term/short-term split). However, the swap exclusion was designed for regulatory purposes, not tax purposes. The IRS has not confirmed that swap-excluded contracts are Section 1256 contracts for tax purposes.\"}]]}]]}],[\"$\",\"details\",\"faq-128\",{\"className\":\"faq-item group mb-2 rounded-xl border border-gray-200 open:border-brand-gold/30 dark:border-slate-700 dark:open:border-brand-gold/20\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"summary\",null,{\"className\":\"flex cursor-pointer list-none items-start justify-between gap-3 px-5 py-4 font-sans text-[15.5px] font-semibold text-brand-blue hover:text-brand-gold dark:text-slate-100 dark:hover:text-brand-gold\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"span\",null,{\"children\":\"Does Polymarket's CFTC-regulated status help with tax treatment?\"}],[\"$\",\"svg\",null,{\"xmlns\":\"http://www.w3.org/2000/svg\",\"width\":24,\"height\":24,\"viewBox\":\"0 0 24 24\",\"fill\":\"none\",\"stroke\":\"currentColor\",\"strokeWidth\":2,\"strokeLinecap\":\"round\",\"strokeLinejoin\":\"round\",\"className\":\"lucide lucide-chevron-down faq-chevron mt-0.5 h-4 w-4 flex-shrink-0 text-brand-muted transition-transform duration-200\",\"aria-hidden\":\"true\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"path\",\"qrunsl\",{\"d\":\"m6 9 6 6 6-6\"}],\"$undefined\"]}]]}],[\"$\",\"div\",null,{\"className\":\"border-t border-gray-100 px-5 pb-5 pt-4 dark:border-slate-700/50\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"p\",\"faqp-127\",{\"className\":\"text-[15px] leading-[1.7] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300 [\u0026:not(:last-child)]:mb-2\",\"children\":\"Polymarket is not CFTC-regulated in the same way Kalshi is. Polymarket is a DeFi platform based in the Bahamas with no explicit CFTC authorization. The Section 1256 argument is significantly weaker for Polymarket trades. Approach 2 (gambling) or Approach 3 (ordinary income) is more defensible for Polymarket.\"}]]}]]}],[\"$\",\"details\",\"faq-130\",{\"className\":\"faq-item group mb-2 rounded-xl border border-gray-200 open:border-brand-gold/30 dark:border-slate-700 dark:open:border-brand-gold/20\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"summary\",null,{\"className\":\"flex cursor-pointer list-none items-start justify-between gap-3 px-5 py-4 font-sans text-[15.5px] font-semibold text-brand-blue hover:text-brand-gold dark:text-slate-100 dark:hover:text-brand-gold\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"span\",null,{\"children\":\"How does NJ tax prediction market income?\"}],[\"$\",\"svg\",null,{\"xmlns\":\"http://www.w3.org/2000/svg\",\"width\":24,\"height\":24,\"viewBox\":\"0 0 24 24\",\"fill\":\"none\",\"stroke\":\"currentColor\",\"strokeWidth\":2,\"strokeLinecap\":\"round\",\"strokeLinejoin\":\"round\",\"className\":\"lucide lucide-chevron-down faq-chevron mt-0.5 h-4 w-4 flex-shrink-0 text-brand-muted transition-transform duration-200\",\"aria-hidden\":\"true\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"path\",\"qrunsl\",{\"d\":\"m6 9 6 6 6-6\"}],\"$undefined\"]}]]}],[\"$\",\"div\",null,{\"className\":\"border-t border-gray-100 px-5 pb-5 pt-4 dark:border-slate-700/50\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"p\",\"faqp-129\",{\"className\":\"text-[15px] leading-[1.7] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300 [\u0026:not(:last-child)]:mb-2\",\"children\":[\"NJ has not issued specific guidance on prediction market taxation. If NJ classifies prediction markets as gambling, winnings are taxed as ordinary income at rates from 1.4% to 10.75%. NJ allows full netting of gambling wins and losses (unlike federal, which caps at 90% for 2026+). If NJ classifies them as capital gains, NJ taxes all capital gains at ordinary rates with no preferential long-term rate and does not allow capital loss carryforward. \",[\"$\",\"a\",\"a449\",{\"href\":\"/post/how-nj-taxes-gambling\",\"className\":\"text-brand-gold underline transition-colors hover:text-brand-gold/80\",\"children\":\"Read more about NJ gambling tax rules\"}]]}]]}]]}],[\"$\",\"details\",\"faq-132\",{\"className\":\"faq-item group mb-2 rounded-xl border border-gray-200 open:border-brand-gold/30 dark:border-slate-700 dark:open:border-brand-gold/20\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"summary\",null,{\"className\":\"flex cursor-pointer list-none items-start justify-between gap-3 px-5 py-4 font-sans text-[15.5px] font-semibold text-brand-blue hover:text-brand-gold dark:text-slate-100 dark:hover:text-brand-gold\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"span\",null,{\"children\":\"Do I have FBAR or FATCA obligations from Polymarket?\"}],[\"$\",\"svg\",null,{\"xmlns\":\"http://www.w3.org/2000/svg\",\"width\":24,\"height\":24,\"viewBox\":\"0 0 24 24\",\"fill\":\"none\",\"stroke\":\"currentColor\",\"strokeWidth\":2,\"strokeLinecap\":\"round\",\"strokeLinejoin\":\"round\",\"className\":\"lucide lucide-chevron-down faq-chevron mt-0.5 h-4 w-4 flex-shrink-0 text-brand-muted transition-transform duration-200\",\"aria-hidden\":\"true\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"path\",\"qrunsl\",{\"d\":\"m6 9 6 6 6-6\"}],\"$undefined\"]}]]}],[\"$\",\"div\",null,{\"className\":\"border-t border-gray-100 px-5 pb-5 pt-4 dark:border-slate-700/50\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"p\",\"faqp-131\",{\"className\":\"text-[15px] leading-[1.7] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300 [\u0026:not(:last-child)]:mb-2\",\"children\":\"Potentially. If you hold funds on Polymarket (which operates from the Bahamas and uses foreign-based smart contracts), you may have FBAR (FinCEN 114) filing obligations if the aggregate value of your foreign financial accounts exceeds $10,000 at any point during the year. FATCA (Form 8938) may also apply if your foreign financial assets exceed the reporting threshold ($50,000 for domestic filers, $200,000 for foreign residents). Consult a CPA or tax attorney if you have significant Polymarket holdings.\"}]]}]]}],[\"$\",\"details\",\"faq-134\",{\"className\":\"faq-item group mb-2 rounded-xl border border-gray-200 open:border-brand-gold/30 dark:border-slate-700 dark:open:border-brand-gold/20\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"summary\",null,{\"className\":\"flex cursor-pointer list-none items-start justify-between gap-3 px-5 py-4 font-sans text-[15.5px] font-semibold text-brand-blue hover:text-brand-gold dark:text-slate-100 dark:hover:text-brand-gold\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"span\",null,{\"children\":\"Is NJ going to ban prediction markets?\"}],[\"$\",\"svg\",null,{\"xmlns\":\"http://www.w3.org/2000/svg\",\"width\":24,\"height\":24,\"viewBox\":\"0 0 24 24\",\"fill\":\"none\",\"stroke\":\"currentColor\",\"strokeWidth\":2,\"strokeLinecap\":\"round\",\"strokeLinejoin\":\"round\",\"className\":\"lucide lucide-chevron-down faq-chevron mt-0.5 h-4 w-4 flex-shrink-0 text-brand-muted transition-transform duration-200\",\"aria-hidden\":\"true\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"path\",\"qrunsl\",{\"d\":\"m6 9 6 6 6-6\"}],\"$undefined\"]}]]}],[\"$\",\"div\",null,{\"className\":\"border-t border-gray-100 px-5 pb-5 pt-4 dark:border-slate-700/50\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"p\",\"faqp-133\",{\"className\":\"text-[15px] leading-[1.7] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300 [\u0026:not(:last-child)]:mb-2\",\"children\":\"As of March 2026, NJ has not banned prediction markets. However, NJ's Division of Gaming Enforcement has indicated it is monitoring the space. NJ's strict gambling regulatory framework means prediction markets could face additional regulation or licensing requirements in the future. This does not change your current tax obligations.\"}]]}]]}],[\"$\",\"details\",\"faq-136\",{\"className\":\"faq-item group mb-2 rounded-xl border border-gray-200 open:border-brand-gold/30 dark:border-slate-700 dark:open:border-brand-gold/20\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"summary\",null,{\"className\":\"flex cursor-pointer list-none items-start justify-between gap-3 px-5 py-4 font-sans text-[15.5px] font-semibold text-brand-blue hover:text-brand-gold dark:text-slate-100 dark:hover:text-brand-gold\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"span\",null,{\"children\":\"Are prediction markets legal in my state?\"}],[\"$\",\"svg\",null,{\"xmlns\":\"http://www.w3.org/2000/svg\",\"width\":24,\"height\":24,\"viewBox\":\"0 0 24 24\",\"fill\":\"none\",\"stroke\":\"currentColor\",\"strokeWidth\":2,\"strokeLinecap\":\"round\",\"strokeLinejoin\":\"round\",\"className\":\"lucide lucide-chevron-down faq-chevron mt-0.5 h-4 w-4 flex-shrink-0 text-brand-muted transition-transform duration-200\",\"aria-hidden\":\"true\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"path\",\"qrunsl\",{\"d\":\"m6 9 6 6 6-6\"}],\"$undefined\"]}]]}],[\"$\",\"div\",null,{\"className\":\"border-t border-gray-100 px-5 pb-5 pt-4 dark:border-slate-700/50\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"p\",\"faqp-135\",{\"className\":\"text-[15px] leading-[1.7] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300 [\u0026:not(:last-child)]:mb-2\",\"children\":\"Legality varies by state. Kalshi is legal in most states as a CFTC-regulated exchange. Polymarket's legal status is unclear in many jurisdictions. Robinhood Prediction Markets is available in states where Robinhood operates but may be restricted in some states. Check your state's gambling and securities regulations. Legality does not affect taxability -- even if your state restricts prediction markets, any gains are still taxable.\"}]]}]]}],[\"$\",\"details\",\"faq-138\",{\"className\":\"faq-item group mb-2 rounded-xl border border-gray-200 open:border-brand-gold/30 dark:border-slate-700 dark:open:border-brand-gold/20\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"summary\",null,{\"className\":\"flex cursor-pointer list-none items-start justify-between gap-3 px-5 py-4 font-sans text-[15.5px] font-semibold text-brand-blue hover:text-brand-gold dark:text-slate-100 dark:hover:text-brand-gold\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"span\",null,{\"children\":\"Should I file Form 8275?\"}],[\"$\",\"svg\",null,{\"xmlns\":\"http://www.w3.org/2000/svg\",\"width\":24,\"height\":24,\"viewBox\":\"0 0 24 24\",\"fill\":\"none\",\"stroke\":\"currentColor\",\"strokeWidth\":2,\"strokeLinecap\":\"round\",\"strokeLinejoin\":\"round\",\"className\":\"lucide lucide-chevron-down faq-chevron mt-0.5 h-4 w-4 flex-shrink-0 text-brand-muted transition-transform duration-200\",\"aria-hidden\":\"true\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"path\",\"qrunsl\",{\"d\":\"m6 9 6 6 6-6\"}],\"$undefined\"]}]]}],[\"$\",\"div\",null,{\"className\":\"border-t border-gray-100 px-5 pb-5 pt-4 dark:border-slate-700/50\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"p\",\"faqp-137\",{\"className\":\"text-[15px] leading-[1.7] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300 [\u0026:not(:last-child)]:mb-2\",\"children\":\"If you're taking a position that differs from the IRS's likely interpretation (e.g., using Section 1256 for prediction markets), filing Form 8275 (Disclosure Statement) or Form 8275-R (Regulation Disclosure Statement) can protect you from accuracy-related penalties if the IRS later disagrees. The form signals that you've considered the issue and taken a good-faith position. I recommend filing Form 8275 if you use Approach 1 or any position that isn't straightforward ordinary income reporting.\"}]]}]]}],[\"$\",\"details\",\"faq-140\",{\"className\":\"faq-item group mb-2 rounded-xl border border-gray-200 open:border-brand-gold/30 dark:border-slate-700 dark:open:border-brand-gold/20\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"summary\",null,{\"className\":\"flex cursor-pointer list-none items-start justify-between gap-3 px-5 py-4 font-sans text-[15.5px] font-semibold text-brand-blue hover:text-brand-gold dark:text-slate-100 dark:hover:text-brand-gold\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"span\",null,{\"children\":\"Should I wait to file until the IRS issues guidance?\"}],[\"$\",\"svg\",null,{\"xmlns\":\"http://www.w3.org/2000/svg\",\"width\":24,\"height\":24,\"viewBox\":\"0 0 24 24\",\"fill\":\"none\",\"stroke\":\"currentColor\",\"strokeWidth\":2,\"strokeLinecap\":\"round\",\"strokeLinejoin\":\"round\",\"className\":\"lucide lucide-chevron-down faq-chevron mt-0.5 h-4 w-4 flex-shrink-0 text-brand-muted transition-transform duration-200\",\"aria-hidden\":\"true\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"path\",\"qrunsl\",{\"d\":\"m6 9 6 6 6-6\"}],\"$undefined\"]}]]}],[\"$\",\"div\",null,{\"className\":\"border-t border-gray-100 px-5 pb-5 pt-4 dark:border-slate-700/50\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"p\",\"faqp-139\",{\"className\":\"text-[15px] leading-[1.7] text-brand-body dark:text-slate-300 [\u0026:not(:last-child)]:mb-2\",\"children\":\"No. You have a filing obligation regardless of whether the IRS has issued specific guidance. File by the deadline (or file an extension). Use the most defensible approach for your situation. If the IRS later issues guidance that changes the correct treatment, you can file an amended return.
